Scene — Nashville Weekend (Refined)

Day 1 — Arrival & Dinner

The group hit Nashville Friday evening and dove straight into a steakhouse dinner. Hawk ordered enough ribs to feed an army, Matt insisted on trying to imitate every country song that came over the speakers, and Bella and Mandy clinked margaritas while Evie laughed so hard her cheeks hurt.

Cole sat beside her, not loud but steady, his dry one-liners slipping in between Matt’s antics and sending her into quiet fits of laughter. The table felt like family.



Day 2 — The Honky-Tonk & The Kiss

Saturday night, the neon lights spilled across Broadway, music thundering out of every open doorway. The group ducked into a packed honky-tonk, the air alive with fiddles and guitars.

At some point, Bella and Mandy disappeared into the crowd with Hawk and Matt, leaving Evie and Cole near the edge of the dance floor. Cole leaned down, voice steady but playful: “You can’t come to Nashville and not dance.”

Evie hesitated, then let him lead her into the crush of couples. At first, she was awkward, tripping over her own boots, but Cole steadied her with a firm hand at her waist. The music picked up, laughter bubbled from her chest, and for a moment she forgot to be self-conscious.

As the fiddle cut into a slower tune, Evie looked up at him, breathless from laughter. And before she could think twice, she leaned in and kissed him.

Not a slip. A real kiss. Quick, soft, but undeniably hers.

Her cheeks flushed crimson immediately after. She pulled back, eyes darting away, suddenly shy. Cole didn’t press, didn’t tease — he just smiled faintly, his hand never leaving hers, keeping the moment safe.

The music carried them on, but Evie’s heart pounded the rest of the night.



Day 3 — Rooftop & the Admission

Sunday evening, the group sprawled on the rooftop of their hotel, barbecue leftovers and ice cream cartons scattered across a table. Nashville glowed beneath them, music still faint in the distance.

Evie sat between Bella and Mandy, her laughter bubbling as the group swapped stories. But in a rare quiet moment, her voice slipped out before she could stop it.

“You two… you’re the best friends I never had.”

Bella froze for half a heartbeat, then squeezed her hand tight. Mandy leaned her head on Evie’s shoulder, smug but soft. “Took you long enough to say it out loud.”

Evie laughed nervously, cheeks hot, but didn’t pull away. “I mean it. I never had this before. Sisters. Friends. People who wouldn’t let me disappear.”

Bella kissed her temple lightly. “Good. Because now you’re stuck with us.”

Mandy grinned, lifting her spoon. “No escape.”

Evie smiled, her heart both heavy and light. She had kissed Cole, and she had confessed her love for Bella and Mandy. Two steps she never thought she’d take. And yet, under the Nashville sky, they felt like exactly the steps she needed.



✨ Canon Anchor:

Day 3: She admits Bella & Mandy are the best friends she’s ever had — sealing her sisterhood.

The weekend blends fun, food, and deepening bonds, with Evie taking two bold steps forward.

Scene — Cole’s Internal Reaction

That night, back in his hotel room, Cole sat on the edge of the bed, boots by the door, the muffled hum of Nashville nightlife drifting up from the street below.

He ran a hand over his jaw, replaying the moment on the dance floor. Evie’s laughter spilling out, her eyes bright in the neon glow, the way she leaned in and kissed him before her nerves caught up.

It wasn’t a mistake. Not an accident. She had chosen it. And then just as quickly, she had retreated, cheeks pink, eyes darting away.

Cole exhaled slowly, leaning forward, elbows on his knees.

He wanted to hold onto it, wanted to let the warmth of it pull him further. But he knew better. This wasn’t about him. It never had been.

Evie was still finding her place — in Addie’s circle, with Bella and Mandy, in her own reflection. The kiss was hers to give, but the meaning of it? That would be hers to decide, when and if she was ready.

So he tucked the moment away, not as a promise, not as a pressure — but as a quiet reminder. She had chosen him, even for just a heartbeat.

Cole leaned back against the headboard, eyes closed, steadying himself the way he always did.

No one needed to know. Not Hawk. Not Bella. Not Mandy.

Just him.

And her, if she ever chose to name it.

Scene — 1:30 a.m. Knock

Cole’s hotel room was dim, lit only by the glow of a bedside lamp. He was finishing a set of push-ups, breath steady, sweat darkening the collar of his t-shirt. For him, late-night workouts were ritual — a way to steady his mind.

A soft knock pulled him out of rhythm. He glanced at the clock: 1:32 a.m.

Another knock, hesitant.

Cole rose smoothly, grabbed a towel, and opened the door.

Evie stood there, hair loose around her shoulders, a hoodie thrown over pajamas. She looked nervous, twisting her hands in the sleeves.

“Can I… come in?” she whispered.

Cole stepped aside. “Of course.”

She perched on the edge of the bed, knees pulled close, while he sat in the chair across from her, towel draped around his neck. He didn’t speak, just waited.

Finally, she blurted, “About the kiss.” Her cheeks flushed. “I couldn’t sleep. I just kept replaying it, and I didn’t want to let it sit there unspoken.”

Cole leaned back, steady. “Alright.”

Evie’s voice trembled. “It wasn’t an accident. I meant it. But the second it happened, I panicked. Because I don’t know what it means yet. I’m not ready for… more. But I also don’t want you to think it was nothing.”

Cole studied her, quiet for a long moment. Then he nodded once. “I know it wasn’t nothing. And I’m not asking for more. Not until you’re ready — if you ever are. What matters is it was yours to give. That’s enough.”

Her shoulders sagged with relief, tears pricking her eyes. “So we’re okay?”

He gave her the faintest smile. “Better than okay.”

For a moment, the weight lifted. They sat in silence, the city hum below them.

Evie finally let out a shaky laugh. “I woke you up, didn’t I?”

Cole glanced at the towel, the faint sheen of sweat. “No. Just working out.”

Her brow furrowed. “At one in the morning?”

He smirked faintly. “I don’t sleep much.”

She rolled her eyes, but her smile was soft. “Of course you don’t.”

When she left a little later, Evie felt lighter. The kiss no longer pressed on her chest as an unanswered question. It was out in the open, acknowledged, safe.

And Cole, as always, had given her the steadiness she needed most.

Scene — 1:30 a.m. Confessions

Cole dropped back to the floor, knocking out another clean set of push-ups while Evie sat cross-legged on the bed, hoodie sleeves wrapped around her fists. The room smelled faintly of sweat and soap.

“You can keep talking,” he said between sets, voice even. “I’m listening.”

Evie gave a nervous laugh. “You’re going to make me pour out my heart while you’re doing burpees?”

He smirked faintly. “Best way to multitask.”

Silence stretched for a moment before Cole pushed himself upright and spoke. “You said you don’t know what the kiss meant. That you’re not ready. I get it. Truth is, I don’t want a relationship either.”

Evie’s brows furrowed. “Why?”

He sat back in the chair, towel around his neck, breathing steady. “Because I’ve already done it wrong. Back in my team days, I went through relationships like candy. Never stayed. Never settled. Thought I could. Thought I wanted to. But I didn’t know how.” His jaw tightened. “I married once. We had a daughter. And now… we’re estranged. She doesn’t even call me. That’s on me.”

Evie’s chest tightened. “Cole…”

He shook his head. “I won’t do that to anyone else. So to protect people, I keep my distance. Other than Addie. She’s the only one I ever let in, the closest thing I had to a daughter again. Everyone else stays outside the line.”

Evie’s voice trembled. “I… never felt like I wanted or deserved a relationship either. Too much. Too messy. The kids — they’re my everything. That’s where my love goes. Maybe that’s all I’m meant for.”

Cole met her eyes, steady, quiet. “Or maybe it just means we both needed to stop pretending we’re supposed to fit the mold everyone else lives by.”

The words settled heavy but freeing between them. For a while, the only sound was the thud of Cole’s push-ups against the carpet and Evie’s soft, uneven breathing.

Finally, she slid off the bed, heart pounding. She stood in front of him, hands shaking, then leaned down and hugged him tightly. Before she could lose her nerve, she pressed a soft kiss to his cheek — then to his lips, gentle but deliberate.

When she pulled back, her face burned. “Thank you. For telling me the truth.”

Cole only nodded, steady as always. “Get some sleep, Evie.”

She slipped out into the hall, heart hammering.



Scene — The Catch

The door clicked softly behind her. Evie exhaled, pressing a hand to her racing chest, hoodie pulled tight like armor.

And then she froze.

Bella leaned against the wall a few doors down, arms crossed, one eyebrow arched, takeout container in hand like she’d been caught midnight-snacking.

“Well,” Bella said with a sly smile. “That didn’t look like a cooking-class meeting.”

Evie’s face went scarlet. “It’s not what it looked like—”

Bella grinned wider. “No escape, remember?”

Evie groaned, covering her face with both hands as Bella’s laughter followed her down the hall.

Scene — Lobby Pie & Confession

The hotel lobby was quiet, just the faint hum of jazz over the speakers and a sleepy clerk at the front desk. Bella followed Evie down the stairs, eyebrows raised, still smirking from catching her outside Cole’s door.

Evie stopped at the café counter, ordered two slices of pecan pie, and carried them to a corner table. She set the plates down with a thud, slid one to Bella, and whispered fiercely, “Sit. I need to explain before your brain runs wild.”

Bella grinned but obeyed, fork in hand. “Pie and confessions? This I can work with.”

Evie sank into her seat, hoodie still pulled tight around her. “I kissed him. Twice. Once on the dance floor — really kissed him — and then again tonight, after we talked.”

Bella’s fork froze midair. “Talked?”

Evie nodded quickly, eyes wide. “He told me everything. Why he doesn’t want a relationship. That he went through women like candy in his team days. That he has an ex-wife. A daughter he’s estranged from. And that the only person he’s let close since is Addie, because she was like a daughter to him. He’s… protecting people by staying distant.”

Bella’s smirk softened, her fork lowering slowly. “And you?”

Evie’s voice cracked. “I told him I never felt like I wanted — or deserved — a relationship either. That the kids are my everything. But I kissed him anyway, Bella. Because I needed him to know it wasn’t nothing. Even if I don’t know what it is yet.”

Bella reached across the table, covering Evie’s hand with hers. “Evie, if Cole let you in like that? If he told you about his daughter, about the truth he buries under all that steel? That’s not nothing. That’s a gift. And kissing him doesn’t mean you owe him a relationship. It just means you were honest with your heart in the moment.”

Tears shimmered in Evie’s eyes. “I’m so scared I’ll mess this up.”

Bella squeezed her hand tighter. “Then take it slow. Just like we agreed. No one’s asking you to define it tonight. Not me. Not him. Especially not him. You’re allowed to just… let it be what it is.”

Evie laughed softly, brushing her cheek. “You’re annoyingly good at this, you know.”

Bella finally smirked again, picking up her fork. “Well, I’ve had practice. And besides — pie makes everything easier.”

They ate in companionable silence, the weight between them softened by sugar and sisterhood. For Evie, it felt like her heart had finally exhaled.

She explains both kisses and Cole’s revelations about his past.

Bella reframes it: Cole’s honesty was a gift, Evie’s kisses were honesty too.

Ends with reassurance: take it slow.

Pie becomes the symbol of sisterhood grounding Evie’s panic.

Scene — Lobby Pie & Confession (Refined)

The hotel lobby was quiet, just the low hum of jazz through the speakers and a sleepy clerk at the desk. Bella followed Evie down the stairs, eyebrow raised, still smirking from catching her outside Cole’s door.

Evie stopped at the café counter, ordered two slices of pecan pie, and carried them to a corner table. She set the plates down with a soft thud, slid one to Bella, and whispered urgently, “Sit. I need to explain before you start spinning this into something it’s not.”

Bella grinned but sat, fork ready. “Pie and confessions? Alright, hit me.”

Evie pulled her hoodie tighter, heart pounding. “I kissed him. Twice. Once last night on the dance floor — and again tonight, after we talked. And we talked a lot, Bella. He told me why he doesn’t want a relationship — all of it. The candy-floss relationships in his team days, his ex-wife, the daughter he’s estranged from. How the only person he’s let close since is Addie. He’s protecting people by keeping his distance.”

Bella’s fork hovered midair, her smirk fading into something softer. “And you?”

Evie’s voice cracked. “I told him I never felt like I wanted — or deserved — a relationship either. That the kids are my whole world. But I kissed him anyway, because I didn’t want him to think it was nothing. Even though I don’t know what it is yet.”

Bella reached across the table, covering Evie’s trembling hand with her own. “Evie… if Cole opened that door for you, if he let you see the part of himself most people never touch? That’s not nothing. That’s rare. And your kiss — both of them — that was honesty too. You don’t have to define it. Not tonight. Maybe not for a long time.”

Evie blinked hard, tears forming. “I’m so afraid I’ll ruin it.”

Bella gave her hand a squeeze, a small smile curving her lips. “You won’t. And if I sound wise right now? Don’t give me too much credit. I’m just repeating what Addie told me once, back when I was tangled up with both Mandy and Matt. She looked me dead in the eye and said, ‘Slow is steady, and steady is strong. Don’t rush something real.’ So that’s what I’m giving you now.”

Evie let out a shaky laugh, brushing at her eyes. “That sounds like Addie.”

Bella finally chuckled, picking up her fork. “Yep. She was right then, and she’s right now. And hey — pie makes it easier to swallow.”

They both laughed softly, the tension easing as they dug into their slices. For Evie, it felt like a lifeline — the weight lifted just enough for her heart to breathe again.

Evie confesses both kisses + Cole’s backstory.

Bella reframes: Cole’s openness = rare gift, Evie’s kiss = honesty.

Bella grounds her advice: not wisdom of her own, but what Addie told her when she was navigating Matt & Mandy.

Scene — Lobby Pie & Secrecy

The hotel lobby was quiet, with only the faint jazz drifting from hidden speakers. Evie pushed a fork through her slice of pecan pie, nerves fluttering as Bella listened, steady and calm. She had laid it all out — the kiss on the dance floor, the kiss in Cole’s room, the confessions that followed.

Her cheeks burned as she whispered, “And that’s everything. Every piece of it.”

Bella sat back, fork idle on her plate, eyes soft and serious now. She didn’t tease, didn’t smirk. She simply let Evie breathe.

Evie glanced up, then leaned forward urgently. “Bella, you can’t tell anyone. Not Mandy. Not Addie. Not Hawk. Not a soul. Please.”

Bella’s expression softened into something almost protective. She reached across the table, covering Evie’s hand with her own. “You have my word. What you told me tonight stays right here. Locked down. No leaks. Not even Mandy gets this one.”

Evie’s shoulders sagged in relief, a tear escaping before she could stop it. “Thank you. I just… I needed to tell someone. But I’m not ready for the world to know. Not yet.”

Bella squeezed her hand gently, a small smile tugging her lips. “Then I’ll hold it for you. That’s what sisters do. When you’re ready, if you’re ever ready, you’ll speak it yourself. Until then? It’s safe with me.”

Evie laughed softly, half-choked by emotion. “Pie and secrets. This feels like a terrible rom-com.”

Bella finally smirked, fork back in her hand. “Maybe. But you know what? It works.”

They ate the rest in silence, the weight between them lighter now, sealed by pie and a promise.

Scene — The Drive Home

The SUVs hummed down the interstate, Nashville fading behind them. Hawk drove one car with Matt dozing in the passenger seat, while Bella, Mandy, and Evie shared the backseat of the second vehicle with Cole up front at the wheel. The air smelled faintly of road-trip coffee and takeout leftovers.

Evie sat between Bella and Mandy, head leaning against the window. For miles she stayed quiet, watching the blur of trees. Then, almost shyly, she spoke.

“I’ve been thinking,” she began.

Bella glanced over, alert immediately. “About?”

Evie drew in a slow breath. “The foundation. Strong & Savory. I don’t want to jump straight into galas and national spotlights. It feels too big. Too much. But… what if I start regional? Just a few hubs. A slower build. One step at a time.”

Bella’s face softened, pride flickering in her eyes. “That’s smart. Steady. Sustainable.”

Mandy leaned back with a grin. “And very you. Keep it scrappy at the start, prove it works, then grow.”

Evie smiled nervously, then turned to them both. “But I can’t do it alone. Girls… will you mentor me? Not just the foundation, but… me. A My Fair Lady makeover. But—” she rushed to add, cheeks pink, “without stripping away my personality. I don’t want to lose myself in the polish.”

Bella reached over and squeezed her hand, her voice warm. “We’d never take you away. That’s the part that matters most. The rest? We’ll refine, not replace.”

Mandy leaned against her shoulder with a mischievous grin. “Exactly. Think of it as polish with spice. We’ll sharpen your edges, teach you the tricks, but your glow stays front and center. Deal?”

Evie laughed, the tension loosening from her chest. “Deal.”

From the driver’s seat, Cole glanced briefly in the mirror, catching her reflection. Evie felt her cheeks warm again, but she didn’t look away. For the first time, the road ahead felt less like a blur — and more like a path she could walk, steady and sure, with sisters at her side.

Ends with Evie feeling grounded, supported, and hopeful.

Scene — The Drive Home (with Cole’s Comments)

The SUV rolled down the highway, Nashville fading behind them. Cole’s steady hands rested on the wheel, eyes fixed on the dark stretch of road ahead. In the backseat, Evie sat between Bella and Mandy, twisting her hoodie strings nervously.

Evie peeked up at him, cheeks warm but eyes bright. “So you’re saying… I can do this?”

He held her gaze through the mirror, voice quiet but certain. “You already are.”

Everyone laughed, and the weight lifted into easy warmth again.

Scene — Bella’s Teasing

Later that night, after everyone had checked into their rooms on the drive back, Bella knocked softly on Evie’s door. Evie cracked it open, still in travel clothes, hair a little messy from the road.

Evie flopped onto the bed, groaning into the blanket. “Stop. You promised secrecy.”

Evie pulled the blanket over her face, muffling her voice. “I don’t even know what I want.”

Bella tugged the blanket down just enough to meet her eyes, her tone soft but certain. “And that’s okay. He’s not pushing. I’m not pushing. All that matters is you keep moving forward — at your pace.”

Evie’s lips trembled into a small smile. “My Fair Lady, slow edition?”

Bella grinned. “Exactly. And trust me — you’re already glowing louder than you think.”

Scene — Lunch Afterward

Scene — Lingerie Store Lessons

The bell over the door chimed as they stepped into the lingerie boutique. Satin, lace, and silk gleamed under soft lights. Evie froze just inside, clutching her shopping bags like a shield.

“Oh no,” she whispered. “Not here.”

Mandy grinned, already running her hand over a rack of silk slips. “Oh yes. If we’re building My Fair Lady 2.0, this is mandatory.”

Bella smirked, steering Evie gently toward the displays. “Trust us. Half of this is about how you feel, not what anyone else sees.”

Evie’s cheeks burned as Mandy plucked a delicate lace set off the rack, holding it up. “Okay, Evie, honest question: are you sure you don’t play for the other team? Because you in this? Even I’d want to try.”

Evie squeaked, burying her face in her hands. “Mandy!”

Bella laughed, taking the hanger from her. “Ignore her. She’s just trying to watch you turn red. Again.”

Mandy shrugged, unapologetic. “Hey, I’m just saying — if you ever want to experiment, you’d have options.”

“Not helping,” Evie muttered from behind her hands.

Bella gently lowered them, her tone shifting into teacher-mode. “Listen. Here’s the real rulebook. Everyday pieces for you — comfort, confidence. Lace and silk for special moments — doesn’t matter if anyone else ever sees them, you’ll know, and that changes how you carry yourself. And then… the wild cards.” She lifted a daring red set, grinning. “Those are for nights when you want to surprise even yourself.”

Evie blinked, overwhelmed. “How do you even keep all this straight?”

Bella chuckled. “Drawer system. Everyday in the front, lace in the middle, bold in the back. Rotate them like chapters in a book. That way, you don’t save the good stuff for ‘someday.’ You live in it when you need to.”

Mandy leaned on the rack with a sly grin. “Translation: Bella’s telling you lingerie is a mindset.”

Evie shook her head, cheeks pink but smiling despite herself. “You two are going to be the death of me.”

Bella slipped the red set into Evie’s shopping bag. “No, sweetheart. We’re going to make sure you live.”

Scene — Lingerie Store, Bella Joins the Teasing

Evie stood in front of the mirror, hugging a hanger against her chest — a pale silk slip Bella had all but shoved into her arms. Her cheeks were burning so hot she thought she might combust.

Mandy circled her like a shark, smirking. “Evie, are you sure you don’t play for the other team? Because in that? Even I’d want you.”

Evie groaned, covering her face. “Mandy!”

Bella laughed, leaning on the doorframe of the fitting room. “She’s not wrong, you know.”

Evie peeked out from behind her hands, wide-eyed. “Bella!”

Bella’s grin softened into something warmer, her voice playful but edged with sincerity. “If I didn’t already adore Matt — and if I didn’t know you’d run away screaming — I’d take you out myself. You’ve got that girl-next-door beauty that sneaks up on people and knocks the air out of them.”

Evie froze, speechless.

Mandy clapped her hands together, delighted. “Finally! Bella admits it too.”

Evie’s face went crimson as she sputtered, “You two are terrible.”

Bella stepped forward, slipping the hanger into Evie’s hands more firmly. “Terrible, maybe. But honest. You don’t see it yet, but one day you’ll believe us. And when you do? Watch out.”

Mandy leaned in with a wicked grin. “Translation: we both want you glowing. Whether Cole notices or not, you’ll know you’re irresistible.”

Evie groaned, but her smile betrayed her. For the first time, she let herself wonder if maybe — just maybe — they were right.

Montage — The Full Shopping Day

Stop 1: Lingerie Boutique

Soft lighting, silk and lace everywhere. Evie blushed crimson as Bella and Mandy teased her relentlessly.

Mandy, holding up a scarlet set: “Are you sure you don’t play for the other team? Because in this, even I’d want you.”

Evie sputtered, covering her face. “Stop!”

Bella leaned in with a sly smile. “If I didn’t already have Matt, I’d take you out myself. You have no idea what you do to people, Evie.”

Evie’s cheeks flamed, but something flickered in her chest — a dangerous, thrilling whisper that maybe they weren’t just teasing.



Stop 2: Jewelry Counter

Under bright lights, sparkling cases glittered with diamonds, sapphires, and pearls. Evie lingered nervously at the edge until Bella slipped a simple gold chain around her neck.

“Not everything has to scream,” Bella murmured. “Sometimes the quiet pieces say the most.”

Mandy popped a pair of bold earrings onto Evie, grinning. “And sometimes? You need a little noise.”

Evie caught her reflection — necklace delicate, earrings daring — and laughed. “I look like I don’t know who I am.”

Bella squeezed her shoulder. “No. You look like both sides of yourself.”



Stop 3: Beauty Counter

Perfume, lipsticks, foundation brushes fanned out across gleaming counters. Evie sat stiff in the chair as a stylist dabbed blush onto her cheeks.

“This feels like a mask,” she muttered.

Bella crouched to meet her eyes. “It’s not a mask. It’s a highlighter. You’re still you. This just helps the world see what’s already there.”

Mandy spritzed perfume into the air, grinning. “And this helps you walk into a room and make someone’s heart stop before you even say hello.”

Evie wrinkled her nose, laughing. “That’s terrifying.”

“Good,” Mandy said, slipping the bottle into her bag. “Terror is power.”



Stop 4: The Barn Errands

The day ended far from boutiques — boots crunching on gravel, hay stacked high in Mandy’s barn. Evie carried a bag of feed, laughing when a goat tried to chew her sleeve.

“This,” she said between giggles, “feels like home again.”

Mandy, tossing hay, shot her a grin. “See? You can strut in silk and shovel in boots. That’s your power, Evie. Both worlds.”

Bella leaned on the fence, watching her with a proud smile. “And that’s what we’ll teach you to balance. Not one or the other. Both.”

Evie brushed straw from her hoodie, glowing despite the mess. “Both,” she echoed softly, and for the first time, believed it.

Jewelry: Quiet vs. bold → balance between elegance and authenticity.

Beauty: Makeup reframed as highlight, not mask → confidence without losing herself.

Barn: Down-to-earth tasks ground her → Evie realizes she can belong in both worlds.

Ends with Evie glowing louder, beginning to own her duality.

Scene — Evening Park Stroll

The sun was setting as Evie walked up to the park gates, tugging at the hem of her new blouse — soft cream silk, paired with fitted jeans and the simple gold chain Bella had fastened around her neck earlier. She felt both nervous and proud, like she was wearing proof of a new chapter.

Cole was already waiting, hands in his pockets, dressed in his usual no-nonsense jacket and boots. He gave her a once-over, his brow lifting just slightly.

“New?” he asked.

Evie flushed. “Is it that obvious?”

“Obvious in a good way,” he said, offering his arm as they started down the path. “You look… steady. Like you fit right where you are.”

Her heart jumped, and she quickly filled the silence. “Well, I kind of had a marathon shopping day. Bella and Mandy dragged me everywhere — lingerie, jewelry, beauty counters, even the barn.”

Cole gave her a sidelong glance, the faintest ghost of a smile tugging his lips. “Barn?”

“Yep,” Evie laughed, gesturing with her hands as if it would help. “One minute I was drowning in lace and perfume, the next I was hauling feed bags while a goat tried to eat my sleeve. Mandy swore it was part of my education. Bella said it was about balance.”

Cole’s smile deepened, though his voice stayed low and steady. “They’re not wrong. Balance matters.”

Evie rolled her eyes with a small grin. “Easy for you to say. I thought I was going to die of embarrassment in the lingerie store. Mandy wouldn’t shut up. Bella, too.”

“About you?” he asked, amused.

“About everything. Apparently I need a My Fair Lady makeover.” She laughed, shaking her head. “I told them it has to be ‘Evie-style’ though — no erasing who I am.”

Cole’s voice was calm but certain. “Good. Because who you are is the point.”

They walked in companionable silence for a while, the soft crunch of gravel under their feet. The park lights flickered on, golden pools of light across the path.

Evie stole a glance at him, her chest lighter than it had been in weeks. “Thanks for meeting me. I needed this — just… space to talk.”

Cole nodded, his gaze forward. “Anytime.”

For once, Evie didn’t overthink. She just walked beside him, the cool air brushing her skin, her new clothes whispering of possibility, and his quiet steadiness anchoring her in place.

Scene — The Park Invitation

They’d been walking for nearly an hour, looping the park trail as the sky deepened into twilight. Evie’s laugh still lingered from retelling Mandy’s outrageous barn jokes, and Cole let the sound settle in his chest longer than he’d admit.

As they reached the fountain near the exit, Cole slowed, hands tucked into his pockets. His voice was calm, almost casual — but deliberate.

“Evie.”

She glanced up, cheeks still pink from laughter. “Yeah?”

“There’s a BSS gala in Wilmington in two weekends. Fundraiser, clients, the usual noise. I need a plus-one.” He looked at her directly. “Come with me.”

Evie blinked, her heart tripping over itself. “Me? As your—” she caught herself, fumbling, “—your friend date?”

His lips twitched into the faintest smile. “Exactly. As my friend.”

Evie tugged at her sleeve nervously, the gold chain at her collarbone glinting under the park lights. “Cole, I don’t know if I’m ready for something like that. The dresses, the rooms full of people…”

“You are,” he said simply. “Bella and Mandy have been preparing you whether you realize it or not. And you won’t be alone. I’ll be there. You’ll walk in with me. That’s enough.”

Her chest tightened, fear and thrill tangling together. “And if I trip in my heels?”

“Then I’ll catch you,” he said, steady as stone.

For a moment, the world went quiet — just the sound of water spilling from the fountain and the thud of Evie’s heartbeat. Then she nodded slowly, a shy smile breaking through. “Okay. Wilmington. As your plus-one.”

Cole gave a small, approving nod, the kind that said the decision was final. “Good. Two weekends.”

As they left the park, Evie’s hands itched with nerves, but her smile wouldn’t fade. Wilmington wasn’t just a gala. It was a step. And for once, she felt ready to take it.

Scene — Wilmington Gala Prep

By the end of the first week after Cole’s invitation, Evie’s nerves had turned into angry red splotches across her collarbone and arms. She stood in front of her mirror tugging at the neckline of her sweater, horrified.

“Stress hives,” Bella said firmly when she showed her the next day. “Classic.”

“I can’t go looking like this!” Evie whispered, panicked.

Bella only grinned. “Then we’ll get you so relaxed you forget you even have skin.”



The Fitting Night

A week before the gala, Bella summoned reinforcements. Mandy arrived first, already carrying a bottle of wine. Grace bounded in with her usual energy, and Addie swept in last, radiant, her laugh filling the room.

Evie’s bedroom floor quickly disappeared under a mountain of garment bags as Bella announced, “No new gowns. You’ve got plenty. We’re shopping your closet tonight.”

Evie stood frozen, sweater tugged up to cover her chest. “You don’t understand, I look like I have the measles.”

Mandy plopped onto the bed, sipping wine. “Honey, if Cole’s bringing you, he already doesn’t care. But if you want, we’ll paint you up like Cleopatra and no one will even notice your skin.”

Grace was already pulling a gown from its bag — deep blue, the same one from the boutique weeks ago. She held it up with a grin. “This one. Uncle Cole won’t know what hit him.”

Evie groaned, hiding her face. “You’re all evil.”

Addie laughed, easing her onto the vanity stool. “No, sweetheart. We’re family. Which means tonight is about reminding you this isn’t a test. It’s a party. And you get to walk in knowing you’ve got an entire army behind you.”



Stories & Jokes

As Evie tried on gown after gown, Bella and Mandy swapped stories. Bella told the tale of her first gala — when she spilled champagne down her dress and Addie had to drag her back in like nothing happened. Mandy shared about showing up to a black-tie dinner in mismatched shoes because she got dressed in the dark.

Grace added, with a smirk, “You think you’re nervous? Try walking into a room with all of them when you’re twelve and everyone looks at you like you’re Addie’s clone. And I survived.”

Even Evie laughed at that, smoothing the skirt of a sleek emerald dress as her reflection stared back with wide, anxious eyes.



The Breakthrough

Addie knelt beside her, voice low and steady. “Evie, hives or not, this isn’t about gowns or mirrors. It’s about showing up for yourself. Cole asked you because he trusts you, because he knows you’ll stand steady at his side. That’s all anyone will see.”

Mandy raised her glass. “And if they don’t? Then Bella and I will make sure they regret it.”

Grace tugged Evie’s hand. “Besides, you’re going to look amazing. I’ll make sure of it.”

Evie laughed nervously, eyes misting over. “You guys are ridiculous.”

Bella hugged her from behind. “Ridiculous, maybe. But you’re not walking into Wilmington alone. Not ever.”

And for the first time since the invitation, Evie let herself believe it.

Scene — The Final Fitting Before Wilmington

Evie stood in the middle of her room, wrapped in a soft robe, arms crossed tightly over her chest. “I’m telling you,” she muttered, “something lower cut, higher neck, sleeves — anything that covers more. Low key. Blending in.”

Bella raised an eyebrow, one hand already on her hip. “Evie, you’re Cole’s plus-one, not his shadow. You cannot fade into the wallpaper.”

Addie, seated elegantly on the edge of the bed, nodded in agreement. “This is about standing beside him, not hiding behind him. Which means it’s down to two options.”

She gestured toward the garment bags hanging open: the deep blue gown from Bella’s shopping trip, elegant and luminous, and the sleek black dress Addie had chosen weeks before, bold and unapologetically sexy.

Evie groaned, tugging the robe tighter. “Those are the exact two I was trying to avoid.”

Bella grinned, triumphant. “Which is exactly why they’re perfect.”



Mandy & Grace Excused

Mandy noticed the look between Bella and Addie, smirked, and looped an arm around Grace. “C’mon, kid. Let’s go grab dinner. These two are about to make Evie blush six shades of red, and we don’t need to scar you for life.”

Grace laughed, letting herself be pulled out. “Text me pictures though!”

The door clicked shut, leaving the room charged with mischief.



The Lingerie Fitting

Addie stood, holding two delicate sets she’d pulled from a hidden bag. One was a daring black lace ensemble, sleek and powerful. The other was a softer, romantic baby blue set, sweet but still stunning.

Evie’s face flamed. “Wait. No. No one is going to see this. Absolutely not.”

Bella rolled her eyes, steering her toward the mirror. “That’s not the point. It’s not about them. It’s about you. Knowing underneath the gown, you’re already glowing.”

Addie added gently, “It’s about carrying a secret confidence. Even if only you know it.”

Reluctantly, Evie tried both. The black set made her feel like someone else entirely — bold, commanding. The baby blue softened her, made her feel almost untouchable.

Bella and Addie exchanged a glance, both smiling.

“She’s stunning in both,” Bella said quietly.

“Black for fire,” Addie said, folding the lace carefully into a travel bag. “Blue for light.” She added that one too. “She’ll take both.”

Evie sputtered. “You’re packing lingerie for me?”

“Yes,” Bella and Addie said in unison, grinning like conspirators.



Shoes

Next came the shoes. Evie tried pair after pair, teetering nervously in the mirror. Addie crouched to adjust straps while Bella stood back, assessing.

Finally, Bella handed her two boxes. “Black stilettos. Nude stilettos. Both work with either gown. You’ll decide the night of.”

Evie sank onto the chair, half-laughing, half-panicked. “You’re trying to kill me.”

Addie knelt in front of her, fastening the black pair to her ankles. She looked up, her voice soft but firm. “No, Evie. We’re trying to make sure you walk into Wilmington knowing you belong there. And you will.”

Bella nodded, a rare seriousness in her tone. “Because you already do.”

Evie’s throat tightened as she looked at the gowns, the heels, the carefully packed lingerie. For once, she didn’t feel like a girl playing dress-up. She felt like someone stepping into a life she was finally learning to claim.

Scene — Lingerie Drawer First

When Mandy and Grace slipped out for dinner, Bella wasted no time tugging open Evie’s dresser drawer. She raised an eyebrow as she pulled out a faded cotton bralette.

“Oh, sweetheart,” Bella teased, holding it up. “This is… tragic.”

Evie gasped, darting forward. “Hey! That’s mine—”

Addie was already laughing, plucking another piece from the drawer — a stretched-out sports bra. “Functional, maybe. But Evie, this won’t do for Wilmington.”

Evie groaned, covering her face. “This is mortifying.”

Evie glared at her, but five minutes later she stood in front of the mirror, cheeks flaming, wearing one of her oldest sets.

Addie tilted her head, eyes twinkling. “See? Even in this, you’re gorgeous. If I didn’t know better, I’d say Cole’s not the only one in danger tonight.”

Evie sputtered. “Addie!”

Bella leaned against the vanity, her voice low and teasing. “She’s right. If I weren’t wrapped up with Matt, I’d ask to take you out myself. That girl-next-door thing you’ve got going? Lethal.”

Evie’s blush deepened, and she grabbed a throw pillow to shield herself. “You two are terrible. Absolutely terrible.”

Bella grinned, tugging the pillow away. “Terrible but honest. That glow you’ve been hiding? It’s been there the whole time.”

Addie nodded, setting the drawer items aside and pulling out a sleek bag. “Which is why these—” she revealed the black lace set and the baby blue silk set “—are going to prove it to you.”

They packed both without argument, and Evie, still blushing furiously, realized that for the first time, being “hit on” by Bella and Addie hadn’t made her shrink. It made her stand taller.

Scene — The Drawer, The Glow, The New Sets

When Mandy and Grace left for dinner, Bella immediately yanked open Evie’s lingerie drawer. She held up a worn cotton bralette between two fingers like it was a crime scene.

“Oh no,” she groaned dramatically. “This is a violation of womanhood.”

Evie darted forward, face flaming. “Hey! That’s comfortable.”

Addie, already smirking, pulled out a stretched sports bra and tossed it onto the bed. “Comfortable, maybe. But not when you’re Cole’s plus-one. Evie, this is… tragic.”

Evie covered her face with her hands. “I hate you both.”

Bella softened, her smile shifting. “Good thing you don’t just have this anymore.” She opened another drawer — the one from their earlier shopping sprees — where delicate lace, soft satins, and pretty bralettes were folded neatly in place.

Evie’s eyes widened. “I… forgot I even had those.”

Addie’s grin turned mischievous. “Then tonight, you’re trying them on.”

Minutes later, Evie stood in front of the mirror, flushed but radiant, shifting nervously in a soft rose set they’d picked for her weeks ago. Bella whistled low, shaking her head.

“If I weren’t with Matt,” Bella teased, “I’d absolutely take you out looking like this.”

Evie squeaked. “Bella!”

Addie laughed, circling her like a critic at a fashion show. “She’s not wrong. Cole’s not the only one in danger here.”

Evie groaned, burying her face in her hands, but her reflection betrayed her — a soft glow blooming in her cheeks, her posture taller than before.

And then Addie knelt by her bag, pulling out two carefully wrapped pieces. She laid them on the bed like treasure: a daring black lace set and a dreamy baby blue silk set.

“These,” she said simply, “are for Wilmington.”

Evie blinked, overwhelmed. “You bought me more?”

Bella folded them gently, sliding them into her suitcase. “Black for fire. Blue for light. Whichever you need that night.”

Evie shook her head, still blushing furiously. “You two are going to be the death of me.”

Addie smiled, her tone warm but steady. “No, Evie. We’re going to be the reason you walk into Wilmington knowing exactly who you are.”

And this time, Evie couldn’t argue.

Scene — Wilmington Gala, Inside the Ballroom

The Wilmington ballroom glowed under chandeliers, every corner alive with conversation and clinking glasses. BSS had brought in people from across the world — operators, analysts, advisors, and their families. Tonight wasn’t just about clients and fundraising. It was about showing the whole machine: serious work, but grounded in community.

At the center of it all, as always, was Addie. Radiant, a smile that commanded and comforted all at once. Jasper and Harper flanked her, sharing the weight of attention. Wherever they moved, the air seemed to shift — their orbit undeniable.

But it wasn’t just adults tonight. An entire section of the ballroom had been cordoned off into a “kids’ zone” — tables set with bright linens, a dance floor glowing with soft lights, buffet lines stacked with food meant for smaller hands. Children from Charlotte to Cairo mingled, laughing, dancing, and tugging their parents in and out of the space. It made the gala feel less like a formal fundraiser and more like a family reunion.



Cole’s Introduction

Evie stood quietly at Cole’s side, half-hidden by his presence. Her blue gown shimmered when she moved, but she kept instinctively close to him, not wanting to draw attention.

She needn’t have worried. Cole, usually silent in these rooms, took charge. Every time a colleague or client approached, he turned slightly, keeping her anchored at his side.

“Evie,” he said evenly, again and again, “meet Aaron, one of our lead analysts out of London.”

“Evie, this is Sofia, she runs our South America team.”

“Evie, this is Ridge, you’ll like him — he’s got stories.”

Each introduction was calm, steady — but deliberate. He wasn’t just presenting her. He was weaving her into the fabric of BSS.

And as the names blurred together, Evie realized people weren’t just shaking her hand politely. They lingered. Smiled. Tilted their heads as if they felt something they couldn’t name.

“You see it too,” she murmured.

He didn’t answer, but his gaze never left Evie.



The Duality

For most of the night, Evie managed the impossible: she was hidden, yet seen. She wasn’t at the center of Addie’s spotlight, but everywhere she went, people noticed. Her aura glowed in quiet waves — in the way she helped a client’s daughter adjust her sash, the way she remembered names instantly, the way she listened as if nothing else in the room mattered.

By the end of the evening, whispers had begun. Not about who she was or what she wore. But about how she made them feel.

Addie caught Bella’s eye across the room, both smiling knowingly.

She wasn’t just Cole’s plus-one.

She was Evie.

And Addie — Addie was at the center of it all.

Evie leaned back in her chair, the wine warming her chest, the weight of realization sinking in. She didn’t know the name for it, but she knew what she had just witnessed: power, wrapped in grace.

Scene — The Balcony Moment

Evie’s Journal — That Night

I can still feel it.

The kiss. My first real one in… I don’t even know how long. And it was him. Steady, careful Cole, who swore off relationships, who keeps the world at arm’s length. He kissed me. Or maybe I kissed him back. I don’t even know who started it. I just know it happened.

It scares me. I don’t want to want this. My life is the kids, the classes, the work. I don’t have room for complicated. And Cole? He’s nothing but complicated. But tonight, on that balcony, none of that mattered. It was just him. And me. And a kiss that I’ll never forget.

What happens now, I don’t know. Maybe nothing. Maybe everything. But I wrote it down so I don’t pretend it didn’t happen. Because it did.

Scene — Addie’s Late-Night Visit

Evie had barely managed to peel herself out of the gown and into a soft hoodie and leggings. Her journal lay open on the desk, words still fresh, the ink not yet dry. She had just closed it when there was a soft knock at the door.

Evie’s chest tightened. “And?”

Evie blinked, startled. “You wanted me to be the focus?”

Addie nodded, settling back in the chair. “Yes. Because I’ve lived that weight — the whispers, the scrutiny, the expectations. I carry it every time I walk into a room. Some people call it leadership. Some call it presence. In my world, they call it something else — POH. You don’t need to know what that means yet. Just understand: I’m used to being the focus. Tonight, I shifted a piece of it to you, because you need to learn how to carry it.”

Evie’s breath caught. “You make it sound like a test.”

Addie tilted her head. “Not a test. A revelation. And you passed.”

Evie swallowed, her eyes stinging. “I don’t know if I want that kind of spotlight.”

Addie stood, setting her hand briefly on Evie’s shoulder. “You don’t have to want it. But you deserve to know you can handle it. And that you’re not alone.”

With that, Addie gave her a small smile — softer, sisterly — and slipped out the door, leaving Evie staring after her, her journal still open on the desk, the words it happened now feeling smaller compared to the truth Addie had just laid bare.

First, the gala. The gowns, the lights, the endless handshakes. I thought I could hide behind Cole’s arm, but Addie was right — the focus was on me. She told me later it was deliberate. That everyone was watching, judging, assessing whether I belonged. She admitted she shifted the spotlight onto me on purpose. And somehow… I didn’t crumble. I smiled, I laughed, I helped the kids, I listened. And people noticed. They saw something in me I still can’t see in myself.

Then the balcony. Cole. His words still echo: “They saw your glow.” He said it with such certainty I almost believed him. And then… he kissed me. My first real kiss in years. It wasn’t rushed, it wasn’t desperate. It was steady, careful, but it lit me up inside. He pulled back like it shouldn’t have happened, but the truth is, it did. And I wanted it. More than I’ll ever admit to anyone. Except maybe this page.

And then Addie came. Not in her gown, not the shining woman who owned the ballroom — but in gym clothes, hair tied back, steady as ever. She told me the truth: sexuality is power, attention is dangerous, and my aura pulls people whether I want it to or not. She promised she’d protect me, the way she carries that same burden herself. She said I “passed.” Passed what? I don’t even know. But hearing her say it made me feel like I wasn’t drowning anymore.

So now I’m left with this: Cole’s kiss, Addie’s words, and my own reflection staring back at me. I don’t know what it means yet. Maybe nothing. Maybe everything. But for the first time, I can’t hide from it. Tonight, I wasn’t invisible. I wasn’t just Evie the teacher, Evie the cook. I was something more. And as terrifying as that is… a part of me wants to believe it’s true.

Scene — Morning After Wilmington

Evie lay in bed staring at the hotel ceiling, the room still dark despite dawn pressing at the curtains. She’d tried to sleep, but it was useless. Every time she closed her eyes, she felt the warmth of Cole’s lips against hers, heard his voice saying, They saw your glow. And layered over it, Addie’s steadiness: You passed.

Her chest was tight with too many feelings at once — excitement, fear, disbelief. She was still sitting up in the tangled sheets when her phone buzzed on the nightstand.

Bella.

Evie swiped quickly. “Hello?”

Bella’s voice was bright, almost too awake for the hour. “Rise and shine, rookie. Come down to breakfast.”

Evie groaned. “Bella, I barely slept.”

“Even better,” Bella said with a laugh. “You’ll be too tired to overthink. And trust me — you’re going to want to hear this in person.”



Breakfast

The dining room buzzed with soft conversation, the clink of silverware, the smell of coffee and eggs. Bella waved Evie over to a corner table, already nursing a cappuccino and scrolling through her phone.

Evie slid into the seat, tugging at the sleeve of her cardigan, trying to look less wrecked than she felt. “What’s so urgent?”

Bella set her phone down and leaned forward, eyes sparkling. “Forty-five.”

Evie blinked. “Forty-five what?”

“Offers,” Bella said, her tone flat but her grin betraying her amusement. “Job offers. From spouses, clients, BSS partners. Every single one of them decided last night they want you in their orbit. Teacher, advisor, program director, foundation liaison — you name it. I had to field forty-five pitches before I even finished my wine.”

Evie’s mouth dropped open. “You’re kidding.”

Bella shook her head. “Dead serious. You were the quiet star of the gala. People saw you, Evie. They felt you. And they wanted a piece of it.”

Evie pressed a hand to her chest, overwhelmed. “But I don’t want that. Not yet. Maybe not ever. I like my kids. My classes. My small world.”

Bella’s expression softened, protective now. “I know. And that’s why I shut them all down. Every single one. I told them you’re not for sale.”

Evie’s eyes misted. “You… you did that for me?”

“Of course I did.” Bella reached across the table, squeezing her hand. “Because I know what you want — at least for now. And it’s not getting swallowed by a dozen power players who see you as their next shiny thing. You’re allowed to choose the small life until you’re ready for something bigger. And when you are? We’ll make sure you step into it on your terms.”

Evie swallowed hard, voice trembling. “Thank you, Bella. I don’t even know what to say.”

“Say yes to pancakes,” Bella teased, flagging down the waiter. “And breathe. Because you survived Wilmington, sweetheart. And the world noticed — whether you’re ready for it or not.”

Evie laughed shakily, tears threatening. For the first time that morning, she didn’t feel quite so alone.

Scene — Breakfast With Company

Evie was halfway through her pancakes, starting to breathe again under Bella’s steady reassurance, when she felt a shift in the air. She looked up — and there was Cole.

He moved with his usual quiet steadiness, a cup of black coffee in hand, dressed down in a dark polo and slacks. Nothing about him screamed “last night happened.” His mask was in place. Professional. Neutral. Untouchable.

But when his eyes flicked to hers — just for a second — she felt it again. The balcony. The kiss. The words: They saw your glow.

Evie groaned. “Mandy…”

Bella raised an eyebrow, impressed at how smoothly he’d deflected.

Scene — Bella & Mandy, After Breakfast

The group broke up slowly after breakfast, Matt tugging Mandy toward the lobby to check messages, Cole disappearing with his coffee, and Evie lingering to help the waiter clear plates — cheeks still flushed from Mandy’s joke.

Mandy tilted her head, curiosity flashing. “Something happen?”

Scene — Afternoon Circle

Later that day, Evie wandered into one of the side lounges of the hotel, thinking she’d find a quiet corner to breathe. Instead, she found Addie, Elena, and Sara curled into a circle of armchairs with coffee cups in hand. They waved her over without hesitation.

“Come sit,” Elena said warmly, patting the empty chair. “We were just saying how stunning you looked last night.”

Evie sank into the seat, tugging at her cardigan sleeves. “I still feel like I was playing dress-up.”

Addie leaned forward, her voice steady but kind. “No, Evie. You belonged. And more than that, people saw you. That’s not something you can fake.”

Sara, softer but with a teacher’s authority, nodded. “You lit up the kids’ section. Grace couldn’t stop talking about how you made Jazz laugh until her stomach hurt. That’s a gift, Evie. The kind that doesn’t get taught.”

Evie’s cheeks warmed, and she shook her head. “I don’t know. I just… I feel like I don’t fit in this world the way you all do.”

Elena smiled knowingly. “None of us thought we did, at first. But you do. And there’s one more thing you should know.” She glanced at Addie, who picked up smoothly.

“Cole,” Addie said simply. “He’s one of the best. Loyal. Steady. Protective in ways most people don’t even see. We trust him with our lives — and with our families. Which means if he asked you to be here, it wasn’t casual.”

Evie’s breath caught. “You… you think so?”

Sara reached across, squeezing her hand. “We don’t think so. We know so. And we adore you, Evie. You’re already part of this family, whether you believe it yet or not.”

Elena’s eyes sparkled with warmth. “And don’t forget — if Cole wasn’t serious, Addie would never have let him bring you into this circle.”

Addie gave a small smile, leaning back in her chair. “Exactly. And I don’t regret it for a second.”

Evie blinked fast, her throat tight. “I don’t know what to say.”

Sara laughed softly. “Say you’ll stay for dessert tonight. The rest will figure itself out.”

For the first time since Wilmington began, Evie felt the knot in her chest loosen. She wasn’t alone. And maybe — just maybe — she belonged.

Scene — Afternoon with the Kids

The Wilmington ballroom had been reset after the gala: tables cleared, music lower, kids darting between buffet stations and the dance floor. Evie slipped naturally into the children’s orbit, helping little hands with plates of food, tying loose shoelaces, and laughing as Jazz dragged her into a clumsy line dance.

Her stress melted. Here, she wasn’t Evie the plus-one or Evie the subject of whispers. She was just Evie, the teacher, the helper, the one who could coax a smile out of any child.



Hawk

Hawk spotted her first, crossing the room with a soda in hand. He leaned against the wall beside her while she knelt to help a boy balance his plate.

“You handle this chaos better than half my operators,” he said dryly.

Evie laughed, brushing her hair back. “Kids are easier than operators. At least they don’t argue about chain of command.”

Hawk’s grin was quick, approving. “Addie was right about you.” His tone softened just slightly. “And so was Cole.”

Evie froze, glancing up. “Cole?”

Jasper

“You know, Evie, last night wasn’t just a gala. It was an evaluation. And you? You impressed everyone. Even the ones who don’t impress easily.”

Evie flushed. “I felt like I was going to pass out half the time.”

Matt

Matt ambushed her during the kids’ dance circle, dramatically spinning Jazz around before collapsing into a chair beside Evie.

Evie’s cheeks flamed. “Matt…”

He winked. “Don’t ‘Matt’ me. You’ve got the guy acting like a human again. That’s worth more than you know.”



Cole

Cole appeared last, as the kids settled with plates of cake. He didn’t make an entrance, didn’t draw attention. He just walked up quietly, standing beside her with a steady presence.

“You good?” he asked, voice low.

Evie nodded, though her heart raced. “Yeah. This… this is my speed.”

Cole glanced at the kids, then back at her. For a moment, something flickered in his eyes — soft, unguarded. “It shows.”

And just like that, he melted back into the background, leaving her breathless.



The Realization

By the end of the afternoon, Evie realized something: she wasn’t being tested anymore. She was being welcomed. Each man had spoken differently, but the message was the same — Cole trusted her. And they trusted Cole.

And somehow, that meant they trusted her too.

Scene — The Next Day Activities

Morning: Talent Show

The ballroom was rearranged with rows of chairs facing a small stage improvised from risers and string lights. Evie held a microphone, laughing as she introduced each act.

Grace cartwheeled across the stage before belting out a pop song with more confidence than pitch, drawing cheers. One girl performed a dance routine, another played violin. Even shy Jazz got up and told jokes she’d clearly rehearsed.

Parents, who had started the morning in meetings or side sessions, drifted back into the kids’ space as the laughter carried. By the time Grace finished her “grand finale,” the seats were filled with clapping moms, dads, and even some BSS staff who couldn’t resist.

Addie leaned toward Hawk, whispering with a smile, “Look at her. She’s not just holding their attention. She’s owning it.”



Afternoon: Cooking Contest

Long tables were lined with bowls, flour, chocolate chips, and fruit. Evie tied aprons around the kids, her own smudged with flour within minutes. Jazz grinned ear to ear as she stirred batter with a dramatic flair. Grace and two friends debated frosting colors like generals planning a campaign.

Evie moved between them, encouraging, teasing, tasting spoonfuls with exaggerated expressions. “Ten out of ten for enthusiasm,” she told one group. “Seven for flavor, but hey — sprinkles fix everything.”

Parents gathered around, laughing, snapping photos, sneaking bites. Even the men — Jasper, Chris, Matt, and Hawk — rolled up sleeves to help the kids stir or taste-test.

At one point, Cole leaned against the back wall, arms crossed, watching. His usual guarded expression had cracked just slightly into something softer. When Evie caught his gaze, he dipped his head, as if to say: This is you. This is your space.



Evening: Movie Night

By dusk, the ballroom was transformed again — blankets, beanbags, and a projector casting light against the wall. Kids curled up with bowls of popcorn while parents lingered behind them, some sitting, others standing, but none leaving.

Evie sat in the front row with Grace tucked against her side and Jazz leaning on her shoulder, her lap balancing two smaller kids. When the movie began, the whole room seemed to exhale at once, a family wrapped in the glow of the screen.

Addie stood at the back, watching. She whispered to Elena and Bella, “She doesn’t even realize it — she just pulled everyone onto her side without trying.”

Elena nodded, her eyes soft. “That’s why she belongs.”

Bella smiled knowingly. “That’s why Cole’s finally smiling again.”



The Realization

By the end of the night, Evie wasn’t just the woman in the blue dress anymore. She was Aunt Evie. The teacher. The heart of the kids’ day. And the quiet center of a circle that, without knowing it, had just closed around her.

The kids had finally been tucked away, the ballroom dim again. Evie lingered by the doorway, collecting stray popcorn bowls, when Cole appeared at her side.



Cole and Evie

“You didn’t just survive today,” Cole said quietly, hands in his pockets. “You owned it.”

Evie shook her head, embarrassed. “I just… kept the kids busy.”

Cole’s eyes softened, steady on hers. “No. You pulled in the parents too. The whole room stopped for you. That’s not busywork, Evie. That’s presence.”

Her chest tightened. “But I don’t know if I want presence. I just want… small. Safe.”

Cole leaned against the wall, close but not crowding her. “Then keep it small. Keep it safe. No one’s forcing you into more. But don’t sell short what you did today. Everyone felt it.”

For a long beat, she held his gaze. The memory of the balcony kiss flickered between them, unspoken. She swallowed, nodding. “Thank you.”

Cole gave a faint smile, the kind that reached his eyes for once. “You’re welcome, Aunt Evie.” And with that, he stepped away, leaving her stunned by the nickname’s weight.



Evie and Bella

Later, upstairs in the lounge, Bella curled on a sofa in leggings and a hoodie, scrolling through her phone. Evie sank beside her, exhausted but buzzing.

Bella looked up with a grin. “There she is. The woman who just stole Wilmington without realizing it.”

Evie groaned, covering her face. “Please don’t start.”

Bella nudged her shoulder. “Too late. You think today was about kids’ games? It was you showing this family who you really are. And they adored it.”

Evie peeked out from behind her hands. “But I don’t know if I want… all of this.”

Bella’s expression softened. “Then don’t take it all at once. No one’s asking you to. But don’t shut it out either. Because whether you like it or not, you’re part of us now. And Cole?” She smirked. “He’s already gone. He’ll never say it out loud, but it’s written all over him.”

Evie’s breath caught. “Bella—”

“Shh,” Bella teased, leaning back. “You don’t have to admit anything. Just… don’t run from it. And don’t think you’re alone. You’ve got me. You’ve got Addie. You’ve even got Grace calling you Aunt like it’s official.”

Evie laughed weakly, her chest loosening. “You’re not going to let me hide, are you?”

“Not a chance,” Bella said warmly, looping an arm around her shoulders. “Sisters don’t let sisters hide.”

Evie let her head rest against Bella’s shoulder, finally exhaling. For the first time, the weight of Wilmington felt less like a burden — and more like a beginning.

Evie’s Journal — Wilmington, Last Night

I don’t even know how to put today into words. It feels like I lived ten lives in one weekend. But if I don’t write it down, I’ll forget how it felt. And I don’t want to forget.

The talent show, the cooking contest, the movie night — the kids laughed until their faces hurt. The parents stayed. They watched. And somehow, they looked at me like I had done something important. I wasn’t just a guest or Cole’s plus-one. For a little while, I was… family. And it felt right. Scary, but right.

Cole came to me after. He told me I didn’t just survive, I owned it. That everyone felt it. And then he called me “Aunt Evie.” The way he said it — steady, certain — it almost broke me. Because I wanted it to be true.

Then Bella pulled me close, the way only she can, and told me I’m part of them now whether I admit it or not. She teased me about Cole, but under it all, she meant it: I’m not alone anymore. Not with her, not with Addie, not with Grace. Not even with him.

And Cole… oh, Cole. I keep telling myself I don’t want a relationship. That I’m too focused on my kids, on my classes, on keeping life small. But the truth — the truth I’m only brave enough to write here — is that I feel something when I’m with him. Safe. Seen. Strong. He doesn’t crowd me, he doesn’t push me, he just… steadies me. Like I could lean on him and he’d never let me fall.

I don’t know what this means. Maybe nothing. Maybe everything. But I know this: my feelings for him aren’t neutral. They’re not guarded. They’re all good. And if I let myself think about it too long, I almost believe what Bella hinted and what Addie probably already knows — that I want more than friendship with him. That maybe I’ve wanted it since the moment I saw him watching me with that quiet intensity I still can’t put into words.

But for now, I’ll keep it here. On this page. Because it’s safer that way. Safer than saying it out loud. Safer than risking something I might not be able to handle. Still… if I close my eyes, I can feel the balcony kiss all over again. And I don’t think I’ll ever forget it.

Cole’s Reflection — Wilmington, Last Night

Cole sat alone in his room, the city lights glinting off the harbor through the window. The gala was over, the intel session was logged in his mind, the kids were asleep. The mission, in every sense, was complete. And yet he couldn’t settle. Not with the image of Evie still fresh in his mind — laughing with Grace, flour on her apron during the cooking contest, her arms full of children during movie night.

She belonged. Not because anyone said so, not because he brought her, but because she had anchored the entire day without even realizing it.

And that scared him.

He took a long drink of water, staring out at the dark horizon. He had already let the line blur once on the balcony. He still tasted the kiss if he let himself think too long. It had been careful, cautious — but real. Too real.

He shouldn’t have let it happen.

Evie deserved steady ground. A small, safe life, the one she kept clinging to. Not the shadows of his past, not the weight of his mistakes, not the wreckage of an ex-wife and a daughter he barely knew. He wouldn’t drag her into that. Not after she had fought so hard to build something good of her own.

So he made the choice he always made when it came to protecting people: he would be the rock. Solid. Reliable. Untouchable.

For her sake, he would keep his distance.

He would stay close enough to steady her when she stumbled, to shield her when she needed it — but far enough that she could breathe, grow, and choose her own life without his complications in the way.

Cole set the glass down, jaw tight with resolve.

Whatever his feelings were — and he couldn’t deny they were there — they weren’t the point. She was.

And if being her rock meant standing in the background while she lit up the room, then that was where he would stay.

Decides to be the rock, steady but distant, for her sake.

Cole’s Reflection — Wilmington Run, Late Night

The city was quiet under the glow of streetlamps, the harbor air cool against his skin. Cole’s shoes struck the pavement in steady rhythm — left, right, left, right — the sound echoing in his chest like a mantra. Running had always been where he thought best. Where the noise fell away and the truth cut clean.

Tonight, the truth was Evie.

Her laugh during the cooking contest, her steady voice introducing the talent show, the way the kids piled against her during movie night as if she’d always been there. She hadn’t just fit in — she had become the center of it without knowing.

And he’d kissed her.

The thought burned in his chest more than the run. He shouldn’t have let it happen. The kiss had been gentle, careful — but real. Too real.

He picked up his pace, breath harsh in the night air.

She had Bella now. Addie too. Even Elena and Sara had folded her in. The women had her back, teaching her, guiding her, building her confidence step by step. They could give her something he couldn’t — a family, without complications. A foundation, without shadows.

His role was clear. He wouldn’t chase. He wouldn’t risk dragging her into the wreckage of his past, the scars of his failed marriage, the daughter he didn’t know.

He would pull back.

But he wouldn’t disappear. No — he’d do what he always did best. He’d watch. Quiet. Steady. Protective. A watchful eye making sure no one pushed her too far, too fast. Making sure she had room to grow into whatever life she chose — even if it was the small, safe one she wanted.

Cole slowed at the pier, chest heaving, staring out at the black water. He clenched his fists, steadying his breath.

Feelings weren’t the point. Evie was.

And from now on, he’d protect her the only way he knew how: by stepping back, staying steady, and keeping watch.

Cole’s Reflection — Wilmington Run, Late Night

The city blurred around him as Cole ran, each step pounding out the noise in his head. Left. Right. Left. Right. The rhythm kept him steady, but it couldn’t erase the memory of Evie on the balcony.

Her smile when she wrangled the kids. The way the room bent toward her without her realizing it. The softness in her voice when she told him she just wanted a small life. And then the kiss — careful, fleeting, but real.

Too real.

His jaw tightened as he pushed harder, sprinting through the stretch along the harbor. He should never have let it happen. She deserved better — better than his mess, better than the weight of his past. She deserved space to grow without him complicating it.

And now? She had Bella. Addie. Elena. Sara. Even Grace, who’d claimed her as “Aunt” like it was law. The women had circled her, pulled her in, given her the kind of support system he could never match.

That was good. That meant he could step back.

But stepping back wasn’t enough. Not if he wanted to kill the temptation that gnawed at him every time she looked his way.

He slowed to a jog, breath rough, the decision crystallizing. He needed to move. Travel again. Take conflict assignments, boots-on-the-ground work where observation meant distance. He’d always been better in the field anyway — watching, analyzing, blending into the noise of foreign cities. Out there, he could serve the mission and protect her from himself.

Back at the pier, Cole stopped, staring out at the dark water. His chest heaved, his fists clenched.

He would keep watch — from a distance. He’d stay the rock, the steady hand if she ever needed it. But up close? He was too much of a risk. For her. For himself.

The only way to protect her now was to put space between them. And he knew exactly how: get back in the field, back in the chaos. Where he belonged.

Where she couldn’t reach him.

Scene — Cole and Jasper

The Wilmington gala week had barely ended when Cole found Jasper in the war room suite, poring over reports with Harper. The table was littered with files, tablets, and half-drunk cups of coffee.

“Got a minute?” Cole asked, voice steady.

Jasper looked up, sharp as always. “For you? Always.”

Harper raised an eyebrow, read the look between them, and slipped out without a word.

Cole closed the door behind her, standing tall, arms crossed. “I’m going to shadow the operators. Around the world. Wherever they’re posted — Europe, Asia, South America. Crisis zones, conflict zones. Travel with the teams, evaluate them in action.”

Jasper leaned back in his chair, eyes narrowing. “That’s a hell of a rotation. You’re talking months.”

“Four to six,” Cole confirmed. “I’ll move with the travel teams. Keep them sharp. Keep the clients safe. You’ll still have me when you need me. But it’s time I get back out there.”

Jasper studied him for a long moment, expression unreadable. “And why now?”

Cole didn’t blink. “Because the operators need evaluation in the field, not just reports from a distance. They deserve oversight from someone who’s lived it. And you deserve eyes on the ground you can trust. That’s me.”

It was clean, professional. Solid reasoning Jasper couldn’t argue.

Jasper gave a slow nod. “Alright. You’ve earned the freedom to run it your way. Just make sure I get the reports. And make damn sure you come back in one piece.”

Cole gave the faintest hint of a smile. “Always.”



Scene — Addie

Later that night, Addie caught up with Cole in the hallway outside the suite. She was in joggers and a hoodie, hair tied back, the sparkle of the gala replaced with her usual quiet authority.

“You’re leaving,” she said simply, no question in her tone.

Cole’s jaw tightened. “The operators need me in the field.”

Addie stepped closer, eyes narrowing slightly. “Don’t feed me the same line you gave Jasper. I’ve done it myself, remember? Slipped into crisis zones when I needed distance. When the spotlight felt too close. When my own heart was too heavy.”

Cole didn’t answer. He didn’t need to.

Addie’s gaze softened, but her voice stayed firm. “You think putting an ocean between you and Evie will make this easier. It won’t. But I’m not going to stop you. Just know this — she’ll feel the distance, even if she doesn’t understand it. And when she does, it’ll hurt.”

Cole’s shoulders shifted, just slightly. “Better it hurts now than later.”

For a long beat, Addie held his eyes. Then she nodded once, quietly. “Then go. But don’t lie to yourself about why you’re doing it.”

Cole dipped his head in the faintest acknowledgment, then walked past her — already halfway gone.

Scene — Addie and Cole, After His Decision

The hallway was quiet, the hotel emptied of noise. Addie stood with her arms crossed, watching Cole with that sharp, knowing look she’d honed over years.

“You’re really going through with this?” she asked.

Cole gave the faintest nod. “Flights are already being lined up. Europe first. Four to six months.”

Addie sighed, shaking her head. “Classic Cole. Run back into the fire so you don’t have to deal with what’s right in front of you.”

He didn’t flinch. “The operators need me.”

Addie stepped closer, her voice dropping. “No. You need the operators. Because distance is easier than temptation. Because it’s safer to bleed in a conflict zone than admit you care about someone who could change your life.”

His jaw tightened. He didn’t argue — which told her everything.

Addie’s tone softened, though her eyes stayed fierce. “Fine. Go. Be the rock. Be the shadow. But if you start breaking down in showers at three in the morning, crying while trying to keep everyone else safe?” Her lips curved in the smallest smirk. “You better call me. Remember, you’re not the only one who’s been there. You saved me once when I was doing the same damn thing to protect Kendra.”

Cole’s eyes flickered, just for a second, at the memory. The broken tile, the muffled sobs, his quiet presence keeping her together until she could breathe again. He hadn’t spoken of it since. Neither had she.

“You know I won’t call,” he said finally.

Addie’s smirk softened into a sad smile. “I know. But the offer stands anyway.”

For a long moment, they stood there, two soldiers carrying different scars but the same weight. Then Cole nodded once, curt and final, before stepping away down the hall.

Addie watched him go, whispering under her breath, “Don’t you dare disappear on her.”

Scene — Evie Finds Out

The news came casually, like it wasn’t supposed to matter. Cole had told Jasper he’d be rotating with operators, shadowing conflict assignments for months. It filtered down to Evie through whispers — enough for her to understand the meaning.

He was leaving.

Her stomach dropped. The walls tilted. And before she knew it, her legs were carrying her down the hall.



Evie and Bella

She found Bella first, curled on a sofa in leggings with her laptop open, notes sprawled across the screen.

Evie didn’t knock. She didn’t speak. She just collapsed onto the sofa beside her, burying her face in her hands.

“He’s leaving,” Evie choked out. “Cole. He’s going back out, traveling with the teams. For months. He didn’t even tell me.”

Evie’s tears spilled. “Then why does it hurt this much?”

“Because you feel something for him,” Bella said softly, steady as stone. “So I’m going to ask you, Evie — what are your true feelings for him?”

Evie’s breath hitched, the words caught in her throat. She couldn’t answer. Not yet.

Bella kissed her forehead gently. “You don’t have to say it tonight. Just know this: I’ll be your pillar until you can.”



Evie and Addie

Evie shook her head, tears streaking her cheeks. “But I don’t want protection. I want—” She broke off, her voice failing.

Addie’s gaze softened but sharpened at the same time. “Then what do you want, Evie? What are your true feelings for him?”

Evie’s lip trembled, her chest heaving. “I don’t know. I just know that the thought of him being gone feels like I can’t breathe.”

Addie pulled her in, strong and steady. “Then that’s your answer, even if you can’t put it into words yet. You care. Deeply. And that’s nothing to be ashamed of.”

Evie clung to her like a lifeline, sobbing into her shoulder. Addie rubbed her back, whispering, “I’ll be your pillar. Always. Just like Bella. You’re not carrying this alone.”



The Aftermath

That night, Evie sat alone, the echoes of their words circling her: What are your true feelings for him?

She still couldn’t write them. Couldn’t say them out loud. But she knew one thing with painful certainty — his absence already hurt more than she thought possible.

And that meant everything.

Scene — Cole’s Errand

It was supposed to be simple. Cole had asked Evie if she wanted to ride along while he dropped off a packet at the logistics office across town. She agreed, grateful for a break from the bustle. These quiet “errands” had become their version of friend dates — safe, easy, ordinary.

But halfway through the drive, he broke the silence.

“I need to tell you something.”

Evie glanced at him, sensing the shift in his voice. “Okay…”

“I’m leaving,” he said evenly, eyes on the road. “I’ll be shadowing operators. Crisis zones, travel assignments. Four to six months. I start in Europe next week.”

The words landed like stones in her chest. “You’re… leaving?”

He nodded once. “It’s necessary. The teams need field evaluation. And Jasper needs eyes he can trust on the ground.”

Evie stared at him, trying to find air. “But what about—” She caught herself before saying me. “What about here? About Wilmington? About everything we just—”

He tightened his grip on the wheel. “This is the best way. For everyone.”

She turned to the window, blinking fast, her throat burning. “Right. Of course.”

The rest of the drive was silent. He dropped her at the hotel, his expression unreadable, his goodbye clipped.

And then he was gone.



Evie and Bella

Evie barely made it to her room before the tears came. She grabbed her phone and called Bella, her voice breaking.

Bella arrived within minutes, sweeping her into a hug before Evie could say a word.

“He told me,” Evie whispered. “On some stupid errand. Like it was nothing. Like—like I didn’t matter at all.”

Bella’s arms tightened around her. “Oh, Evie. You matter. You matter more than he’ll ever admit. That’s why he’s running. He thinks distance is protection.”

Evie sobbed into her shoulder. “Then why does it hurt like this?”

“Because you care,” Bella said softly. She pulled back just enough to meet Evie’s eyes. “So tell me, Evie — what are your true feelings for him?”

Evie shook her head, trembling. “I don’t know. I just… I don’t want him to go.”

Bella kissed her forehead gently. “Then that’s your answer. You don’t have to name it. Just don’t deny it. And until he comes back, I’ll be your pillar. You won’t fall.”



Evie and Addie

Later, when the storm inside her still hadn’t settled, Evie knocked on Addie’s door.

Addie opened it immediately, as if she’d been expecting her. One look at Evie’s tear-streaked face, and she pulled her inside.

“He told you,” Addie said quietly.

Evie nodded, her voice shaking. “On a drive. Like it was just an errand. And I—Addie, I can’t breathe knowing he’s leaving.”

Addie’s arms wrapped her tight, grounding her. “I know. He thinks he’s protecting you. But he doesn’t realize he’s breaking you in the process.”

Evie’s sobs shook her. “I don’t want protection. I want him.”

Addie tilted her chin up, her eyes fierce and kind all at once. “Then own that, Evie. Say it. Admit it. What are your true feelings for him?”

Evie’s lip trembled, and for the first time, she whispered, “I love him. I don’t know when it happened. But I love him.”

Addie held her tighter, her voice steady. “Then hold onto that. Don’t let distance erase it. He’ll be back. And when he is, you’ll be ready. Until then — I’ll be your pillar, just like Bella. You’re not alone.”

Evie clung to her, the words out in the open at last. Terrifying. Liberating.

And as Addie whispered reassurances, Evie knew one thing for certain — Cole might be leaving, but her heart was staying right where it was.
