Scene — Addie Brings Evie to Kendra

The morning after Cole’s announcement, Evie still felt raw. Her eyes were swollen, her chest heavy, but Addie wasn’t letting her isolate.

“Come with me,” Addie said firmly, leading her down the hall to a quiet lounge where Kendra sat with Chris, reviewing training notes.

“Kendra,” Addie began gently, “this is Evie. You’ve seen her around, with the kids, with the classes. But I want you to know her properly.”

Kendra smiled warmly, standing to hug her without hesitation. “Of course. Bella and Grace haven’t stopped talking about you.”

Evie tried to smile, but it faltered. Addie caught it, and placed a hand on her shoulder.

“She’s hurting,” Addie said quietly. “Cole’s decision hit harder than she expected.”

Kendra’s expression softened, her eyes sharpening with understanding. “I know that kind of hurt.”

Evie blinked at her. “You do?”

Addie nodded, stepping back so Kendra could speak.

“Kendra knows,” Addie said, her voice steady, “because she lived through something similar with me. When I was hospitalized, it wasn’t just from the pressure of the work. It was from pouring myself out for everyone else. I thought Kendra needed space with Chris — so I gave too much of myself, too fast, too far. And it almost broke me.”

Kendra reached for Evie’s hand, her grip strong. “And I thought Addie was untouchable until then. Seeing her vulnerable taught me something — none of us get through this without pillars. Chris is mine. Addie became mine again. And now? I can be one for you.”

Evie’s throat tightened, her tears threatening again. “But I feel like I’m drowning. And Cole… he’s just leaving me to it.”

Kendra squeezed tighter. “I know. And it hurts like hell. But you’re not drowning alone. You’ve got Bella. You’ve got Addie. And now you’ve got me. You don’t have to hold yourself up when it feels too heavy. We’ll do it for you until you can again.”

Addie’s eyes glistened, but her voice was steady. “That’s why I wanted you to hear this from us together. Because I’ve been where you are, Evie. I gave too much and nearly lost myself. You’re not going to make that mistake. We won’t let you.”

For the first time since Cole’s words crushed her, Evie felt a strange steadiness in her chest. Fragile, but real.

Three pillars. Bella. Addie. Kendra.

Maybe she could stand after all.

Addie brings Evie to Kendra & Chris.

Addie shares her hospitalization story: giving too much while thinking Kendra needed space with Chris.

ChatGPT said:

Perfect — we’ll build this in two steps:

Kendra’s private follow-up with Evie → practical advice on how to carry herself through Cole’s absence, the soldier’s way of survival.

Addie confronting Cole before he leaves → raw honesty, naming what he’s doing: breaking Evie’s heart.



Scene 1 — Kendra and Evie, Private Follow-Up

Later that afternoon, when the bustle had calmed, Kendra caught Evie in the gym. Evie was folding towels, trying to look busy, her eyes still swollen.

Kendra leaned against the counter, arms crossed. “You’re not fooling anyone, Evie.”

Evie looked down. “I just… don’t know how to do this. How to breathe when he’s gone.”

Kendra stepped closer, her voice steady but kind. “Then let me give you something that works. Structure. Balance. When Chris was deployed, when Addie was flat on her back in the hospital, when everything felt like it would crush me — structure kept me alive.”

Evie looked up, listening.

Kendra ticked points off on her fingers. “One — don’t isolate. People will try to give you space. Don’t take it. Stay with Bella, Addie, me, whoever you trust. Two — keep moving. Train, teach, walk. Don’t let the stillness swallow you. Three — let yourself feel it, but don’t let it own you. Write, cry, scream into a pillow if you have to. Then get up and do the next thing.”

Evie’s lip trembled. “That sounds… hard.”

Kendra’s smile softened. “It is. But you’re stronger than you think. And you’ve got us. We’re your pillars now. Lean on us until you can stand steady on your own.”

Evie nodded, tears falling silently. “Thank you.”

Kendra squeezed her shoulder. “You’ll get through this. And when Cole comes back, you’ll be ready for whatever it means.”



Scene 2 — Addie and Cole, Before He Leaves

Cole stood by the SUV, bag packed, ready for the airport. He adjusted his watch, his mask already in place — calm, controlled, unreadable.

Addie approached, her arms crossed, her gaze sharp. “So that’s it? You’re just going to walk away?”

Cole’s jaw tightened. “This is what needs to be done. The operators—”

“Don’t you dare,” Addie cut in, stepping closer. “Don’t feed me the Jasper line. This isn’t about the operators. This is about Evie.”

Cole’s silence said everything.

Addie’s voice dropped, low and fierce. “You kissed her. You gave her hope. And now you’re ripping it away like it was nothing. Do you understand what that does to someone who’s never believed she was worth more than the small life she built for herself?”

Cole’s hands flexed against his bag. “I’m protecting her.”

“No,” Addie snapped. “You’re breaking her heart. And don’t pretend you don’t know it. I’ve seen her with Bella. With Kendra. With me. She’s shattered, Cole. And all she wants is you.”

His eyes flickered, pain breaking through for the briefest second. “That’s why I have to go.”

Addie shook her head, tears threatening but her tone firm. “You think distance is protection. It’s not. It’s cowardice. You’re strong enough to fight wars, but you can’t stand in front of a woman who loves you? You’re better than this, Cole. You always have been.”

For a long moment, he didn’t speak. Then he picked up his bag, shoulders rigid.

“I’ll send my reports,” he said quietly.

Addie’s voice followed him as he turned away. “Send whatever you want. Just know this — she’ll still be here when you get back. And when you finally realize what you’ve done, you’d better pray it’s not too late.”
